on behalf of the ecr group. – mr president, while some european countries, including belgium, believe that the new financial transaction tax (ftt) will discourage speculative trading and bolster debt-laden public finances, it could in fact sink the euro area into deeper economic trouble. well—judged regulation is important. ill—judged regulation, as is the case with the eu’s proposed ftt, can do immense economic damage. indeed the ftt would have a severe impact on the functioning of debt markets, which are essential for governments and companies to raise funds, as it would increase both their borrowing and operational costs. the commission’s proposal is not just a tax on banks and is not just a tax on speculation, but will impact all areas of business as it will be applied at each stage of a multi-tiered financial transaction. this will have damaging consequences for the overall economy as it will cause a flood of financial activity to move beyond the borders of the ftt zone. as for the non—participating states, the ftt will be charged not just on transactions that take place within the 11 countries, but on any transaction that involves shares or bonds issued by them. it therefore threatens to give investors an extra reason not to buy europe’s securities or transact with its institutions at a time when it can ill afford to drive away economic activity.

member of the commission. – mr president, the commission welcomes mrs ayala sender’s own—initiative report, which draws attention to two important elements of our transport policy: first, the trans—european transport networks – ten-t – and, second, freight logistics. these elements are vital for the efficiency, service quality and sustainability of transport, and therefore for jobs, growth and investment in the european union. i think we are already on track to take up the challenges set out in the report prepared by parliament. multimodality and logistics are strong components of our ten-t policy. trans-european transport corridors support a more integrated, multimodal freight transport and logistics. the ten-t guidelines and the connecting europe facility regulation address a broad range of aspects referred to in our report. allow me to highlight the most important ones. all transport modes are covered. the removal of bottlenecks on railway lines, as well as inland waterways, are identified as investment priorities for european long—distance transport. in the road sector, the focus lies especially on increasing efficiency through innovative technologies, safe and secure driving and parking conditions, as well as the smooth integration into multimodal transport chains. intelligent transport systems are included for all modes in order to enable a more efficient infrastructure use, to promote new—generation user services for passengers and freight, as well as automation and digital solutions. under our ten-t policy, network nodes and the connection of transport modes are given reinforced importance, including at terminals, ports and airports. urban nodes and urban logistics infrastructure are amongst the newest elements of ten-t corridors. they allow investments in efficient multimodality and freight logistics and, thereby, also contribute to the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions. finally, infrastructure for clean fuels, such as natural gas, hydrogen and electricity, forms an integral part of ten-t infrastructure. eu funding under the connecting europe facility supports various types of projects, from large-scale infrastructure to very targeted intelligent transport system projects, clean fuel solutions or access infrastructure to multimodal terminals. while the ten-t corridors are a top priority, attention is also given to the complementarity between the core and comprehensive networks. the core network corridors approach has proven an effective tool to identify obstacles to efficient and seamless freight transport flows. the corridors’ completion continues to require substantial investment to remove physical bottlenecks which still hamper smooth flows of passengers and goods. eu funding until 2020 and beyond, including the mobilisation of private investment through the european fund for strategic investment (efsi), remains a key element to address the investment gap to meet the needs in transport sectors with focus on ten-ts. we estimate the needs to complete the ten-t core network to be eur 700 billion by 2030. thus we need to mobilise all sources of private and public funds. for example, we are going to launch a eur one billion cef blending call next month to combine grant funds with efsi financing whenever possible, and private funds to fund projects in line with ten-t priorities. an efficient and sustainable transport system is key to making full use of the internal market and to generating new jobs and economic growth. remaining administrative and technical barriers in and between modes of transport and national systems should also be removed. once again, i wish to thank and congratulate you on your report. on behalf of my colleague, violeta bulc, the commission welcomes that the european parliament attaches importance to the ten-t and the good functioning of logistics in the eu. we know we can count on your support to connect europe.
written. we are aware of the need to strengthen the ten-t networks and to encourage the real digitalisation of transport with a new safety system and the use of new forms of energy in transport. we certainly have to take into account the humanitarian and migration crises, and the increasing closure of borders, which is particularly problematic for freight traffic. in this context, i believe that logistics is of paramount importance in order to guarantee efficient and sustainable freight transport in the eu. the commission notes that the aid is limited to the minimum necessary to ensure the long-term viability of the project. regional networks should not be left out of the european perspective, including in terms of financial and regulatory measures, and therefore investment is needed to complete the links between the main union corridors. it is clear that the development of transport modes based on electricity and alternative energies, and consequently the necessary infrastructure, must be encouraged.

spheres, such as high unemployment, corruption and poor judiciary, but also in the country's structure, in terms of weak central state institutions, conflict of competence of authorities at various levels, and slow decision-making and frequent blockages at all levels of government. for example, a number of decisions of the constitutional court of bosnia and herzegovina have not been implemented, which leads to the country being in a de facto lawless state, with regard to the functioning of the judiciary and the rule of law. one of such decisions is the decision on the election of members and functioning of members of the assembly of the federation of bosnia and herzegovina, the house of peoples. in the existing arrangements, bosniaks can elect serb and croat representatives, have a majority and form a government without the participation of representatives who are elected by the majority of the other two peoples. it is a mockery of the principle of equality of the three constituent peoples in bosnia and herzegovina, a mockery of the constitution of bosnia and herzegovina, as well as a mockery of the dayton agreement, on which bosnia and herzegovina rests. bosnia and herzegovina is a predominant country. and that is why i think that the european parliament's resolution should be more precise, clearer and more decisive in calling for a solution to all these difficulties that bosnia and herzegovina is facing. because of the importance of bosnia and herzegovina in this area, because of the instability and unrest that threatens the entire area, i call on madam president, croats are bound to bosnia and herzegovina by historical, cultural and kinship ties, and the republic of croatia has the longest land border, obligations as a signatory of the dayton agreement, a constitutional obligation to protect croats in bih, as a constituent people, and significant economic exchange. therefore, i am responsible in stating that no one cares more for the stability, sustainability and prosperity of bosnia and herzegovina than the croatian people and the croatian state. but it won't be good will alone that will make bih function as a state. the dayton formula has long since fallen short of the needs of bosnian society, and separatist and centralistic tendencies are hampering the healing of war wounds and the functioning of institutions. bih must become a decentralized community of three peoples with equal rights who govern themselves, do not threaten others and build mutual trust at the state level. this can only be achieved by a federal model whereby the croatian people will also get what the other two nations already have in practice.

madam president, the tragic consequences of the recent terrorist attacks in france, belgium and germany have highlighted many weaknesses in the existing system of border controls in the european union. there is no practice of exchanging data between members. but the proposed combination of systematic controls on documents and persons in relevant databases will ultimately enable synergy in the structure of the system. we should support proposals that allow for greater security for citizens, but we must stress that this proposal will have implications for the technical, technological, administrative and human aspects of border control. especially at the busiest border crossings during the holiday season. it is appropriate to take into account the need to strengthen the capacity for such checks, as the existing technology is under-capacitated and does not allow for the rapid retrieval of biometric data, its verification in existing systems and its storage in new systems. also, some member states still have border controls within schengen. when we add to this the threat by certain schengen members of blocking the borders during the tourist season to those who are not yet schengen members, under the pretext of implementing this proposal, we see unnecessary problems in its implementation. it is a pity that the final version does not contain some of the high-quality proposals parliament adopted in committees. political reality, however, requires a strengthening of the union's security and that is why i will support this proposal. but we should be careful not to make it difficult for those who are entitled to do so to cross the border. this security instrument must not be misused for political purposes.

madam president, it is undeniable that robots are the future and that they will make many jobs much easier, and they are already doing so. however, what concerns me about this report, and why i am amending it with the support of a colleague, is the creation of a legal status of an electronic person for robots that make autonomous decisions and act independently of humans. right comes from life. robots are the product of human minds and human hands, but they are objects, not persons. they have no life in them, and we cannot give them the status of an electronic person. we can't put humans and robots on the same level. creating a legal status for robots as a cause-and-effect would mean waiving the liability of their manufacturers or owners. this would be a legal precedent that no one knows how to make even remotely feasible. i urge you to vote for our amendments, against the creation of legal status for robots and to make it clear that robots are not equal to and have equal rights with humans.

written. since the signing of the treaty of rome in 1957, which prohibits discrimination on the basis of sex in relation to pay, women have continued to fight for equality and equity in the workplace. unfortunately, the reality is different and shows that women earn on average 17% less than men, which later affects the lower retirement by 40%. i have to point out that the pay gap is heavily influenced by gender stereotypes and prejudices. for example, women are often grouped into sectors that have lower average salaries, lower social standing and are considered traditionally female occupations, such as care work. also, women spend an average of 21 hours a week on unpaid work, such as caring for the home and children, while men spend only 9 hours, with long-term effects on women's careers, earnings and pensions. the problem i want to focus on is the weaker continuity of employment among women. women are less likely to be in permanent employment and to sign long-term contracts because of the responsibilities they have with their family and children. increasing social support and adopting a package of measures to reduce the barriers to working parents would enable women to be mothers without fear for their jobs and for the existence of themselves and their families.

member of the commission. – mr president, i would like to thank members for this morning’s presentation, and the debate and comments that have been made on a subject which, i must say, is actually very close to my heart. in particular, i would like to thank the rapporteur, emma mcclarkin, for her excellent report. as has been pointed out, the scale of illegal wildlife trade and the impact on populations of vulnerable plant and animal species are extremely serious. concerted international action is needed to prevent the loss of such species due to greed and ill-informed behaviour. we are focusing today on trade-related aspects of this fight. at the same time, we should recall the importance of eu action in other policy areas as well, such as the provision of support to developing countries through our cooperation work. the report refers to the trade for all communication, which underlines the importance of a values-based trade policy. i welcome the high degree of consensus that i understand exists between the political groups on the need for action to tackle this appalling phenomenon of illegal wildlife trade, an area of economic activity that is clearly contrary to our values. the key multilateral instrument to address wildlife trade is, as we all know, the convention on international trade in endangered species (cites). cites decisions apply to all cites parties and, furthermore, it has the ability to take action if a party fails to follow the rules. support for the effective implementation of cites is a key feature of our trade policy. it is one of the international conventions that must be implemented effectively by beneficiaries of trade preferences under the eu’s gsp+ scheme. commitments on effective implementation of cites are included in the trade and sustainable development chapters of eu free trade agreements and the commission does not hesitate to raise cites issues directly in our dialogues with trade partners as necessary. today’s report also correctly identifies an important role for the private sector in tackling wildlife trafficking, including businesses in the aviation and shipping sectors. the concept of responsible business conduct is one that is strongly supported by the eu and is increasingly reflected in our various trade agreements. a high—value illegal trade such as that of illegal wildlife creates the conditions for corruption, as has been noted. as announced in the trade for all communication and referred to in today’s report, the commission intends to include provisions to address corruption linked to international trade in future trade agreements. the eu also successfully proposed, at the last conference of the parties to cites, a resolution which explicitly calls for dedicated action against corruption linked to wildlife trafficking. i can assure you that the report will be considered very carefully by the commission, including in the ongoing work on the implementation of the eu action plan against wildlife trafficking, and today’s timely debate has again highlighted this critical issue at the intersection of environmental and trade policy. as many of you have said, it is imperative that we take action to tackle the scourge of this trade. so thank you to the rapporteur and thank you to those who participated in this morning’s discussion.
written. the continued increase in illegal trade in wildlife is threatening biodiversity, destroying ecosystems, and increasing the number of animals and plants that are becoming extinct or endangered. the wealth of fauna and flora is an integral part of humanity and our planet and should not be underestimated, but concrete measures are needed to enable their conservation and wise management. unfortunately, the european union is a huge market for illegal wildlife products, but also an important transit hub. the commission should also be able to provide information on the impact of the measures taken on the environment and the environment protection. i am sure that economic development and the use of natural resources are two things that can and must go together!

written. due to the european union's dependence on imported raw materials, the key challenge is to keep as many resources as possible within the union and to step up efforts to transition to a circular economy. it offers great opportunities and potential for local economies, and all involved would benefit. this would result in significant savings for all consumers and would reduce overall annual greenhouse gas emissions. greenhouse gas emissions could be reduced by between two and four percent per year. the circular economy should be an integral part of climate policy. the commission considers that the aid is compatible with the internal market if it is granted to the beneficiary in accordance with the conditions laid down in article 107 (3) (c) of the treaty. it is necessary to define a clear regulatory framework at eu level. in order to achieve the credibility and comparability of the results of the member states, the definition of municipal waste should be aligned with that established by eurostat and the oecd for statistical reasons. the definition, combined with a harmonised single calculation method for all member states, must be based on data from the final recycling process and supported by an effective reporting system to prevent the disposal of waste landfilled or incinerated from being identified as recycled waste. reuse and recycling are key to creating a new policy to promote sustainability, where waste is transformed from a problem to a resource.

mr president, i congratulate the rapporteur on a job well done, because it is a really complex package of regulations to ensure the quality of food from producer to consumer. the agri-food sector is the second largest sector in the european union. it employs 40 million people who produce 750 billion euros of new value every year. the european union has the highest food safety standards in the world, but recent scandals involving, for example, horse meat in europe and contaminated chicken and eggs in croatia show serious weaknesses in the existing legislative framework. the european union's legislation is fragmented into 70 different regulations. in addition to the increased control of disease transmission, too much room is left for malpractice in shops as customers are threatened by cheap imported products of questionable quality and nutritional value. the paradox is that domestic products are thoroughly tested while products imported from the rest of the european union have a single health certificate, often without coverage. the sale of defective products between member states is absolutely prohibited in the single market. the costs of controls, however high, must not be used as an excuse for endangering the health and life of consumers.

on behalf of the alde club. madam president, i would like to thank the rapporteurs, mr gahler and mr gonzález pons, for a very good report and good cooperation. the main finding of the report is that there are no legal obstacles to the development of a european common security and defence policy, and the main recommendations of the report are the formation of a european defence agency and permanent structured cooperation as the european union's original agencies and institutions on which the common security and defence policy would rest, the creation of european integrated forces and a joint civil and military command that would be permanent. but i would like to point out the limitations that exist, limitations of a legal nature which are not the limitations of the european treaties but of the international world order, and that is that even if we had achieved all the objectives that we have set out in this report, the european union would not have been able to intervene even in its neighbourhood, in those areas that particularly concern it, because it would not have been able to get the consent of the security council, the members of the security council because it would have touched their strategic interests. this is in fact the greatest legal limitation to the development and effectiveness of the common european security and defence policy. i therefore particularly welcome and emphasise the part of the report which calls on the member states to act in the context of the reform of the united nations, and in particular in the context of the reform of the united nations security council, so that the european union can intervene in areas which concern it, such as the middle east, north africa, areas where crises particularly affect the european union, and there is no possibility of intervening and acting in those areas.
mr. president, let me be clear about this. until this european union has a clear defence force, a solid security policy and military strength, we will not be a fully respected partner practically anywhere. we may have good political ideas, we may have peace initiatives, but without serious military force the european union will not be a credible world player to the end. syria is the best example. russia and the united states are at war in syria. they're talking about war and peace, and we're feeling the crisis in syria and we're falling apart because of it. the migrant crisis is tearing us apart. so we must be credible to the end in order to really take advantage of what lies ahead, which is to defend the interests of our citizens and to create a real security and defence union.

perspective of the western balkans. in this spirit, the presidency is committed to advancing the enlargement process in full respect of the basic principles of conditionality and on merit. montenegro remains the frontrunner in the accession process and it made further progress last year. we welcome, in particular, the latest accession conference of 13 december, where it was possible to open two further negotiating chapters: chapter 11 on agriculture and rural development and chapter 19 on social policy and employment. with a view to advancing negotiations, the council continues to pay close attention to montenegro’s progress in the rule of law, which, as per the negotiating framework, determines the overall pace of montenegro’s negotiation process. as the report rightly points out, the legal framework and institutional set-up in this area are largely complete. progress has been registered, but further implementation remains key, building on the results achieved so far and also leading to a strengthened and sustainable track record, in particular in the fight against corruption and organised crime. the need for a continued focus on fundamental reforms is also something clearly expressed in your draft resolution and in the commission’s report. they spell out important challenges for montenegro to ensure freedom of expression and media freedom, to pursue public administration reform, and to strengthen the independence of institutions. on the economic side, montenegro needs to take measures to ensure fiscal sustainability, to control public, and to improve the trade balance and the business environment. advances on the rule of law can make a significant contribution in this regard. whilst progress has been made, there are still a number of reforms which montenegro needs to carry out. 2017 will be an important year for montenegro’s euro-atlantic integration, and we encourage the country to build on the progress already made and to take the reform process forward. the process should not be considered merely as being an accession requirement, but a means leading to a more stable, prosperous and fair country. in this respect, one cannot over emphasise the importance of taking ownership of these reforms, which first and foremost are in the interest of, and to, the montenegrin people. we are well aware that montenegro is currently facing a difficult political situation. it remains important for the government to maintain political commitment to the reform process and for all political parties to re-engage in a constructive dialogue, notably within the parliament. the council also closely followed montenegro’s parliamentary elections in october and took good note of the overall positive assessment by the osce. alleged irregularities in the elections need to be investigated, along with follow-up to the recommendations by international election observers. the clear messages in your draft resolution are well appreciated. i would also like to highlight two points regarding montenegro’s external relations. in the area of foreign policy, the country remains a reliable partner to the european union. let me use this opportunity to strongly commend montenegro for its consistent cooperation in this regard and in particular for its full alignment with the eu common foreign and security policy. montenegro also continues to play a positive role in further developing regional cooperation and in advancing good neighbourly relations. in light of the overall situation in the western balkans, we should continue to highlight this point, as it actively contributes to the stabilisation of the very same region. in summary, montenegro continues to make steady progress in the eu accession negotiations and maintains its good cooperation with the eu and its neighbours in the region. looking ahead, we hope to move the accession negotiations forward under our presidency by opening and provisionally closing those negotiating chapters which are technically ready with montenegro.

for this very well-balanced report. indeed, may i extend my thanks to all here who contributed to this important work. as we have been hearing, montenegro has made steady progress on its eu path. rule further results. montenegro is strongly committed to regional cooperation, as ministers noted, and has good relations with its neighbouring countries. in the western balkan context that is no minor achievement. let me also reiterate our praise for montenegro’s complete alignment with the eu’s common foreign and security policy, as mr borg said. montenegro is a country with a clear eu perspective entrepreneurs, from farmers to consumers.

member of the commission. – madam president, i would also like to thank my colleague, werner kuhn, the rapporteur on this file, for the very clear explanation that he has just given. the commission welcomes the agreement reached between the two co—legislators on our proposal concerning the recast of the regulation on characteristics for fishing vessels. as werner explained, this is essentially a technical exercise consisting in codifying a rather old legal text dating back to 1986 and updating an empowerment to the commission in accordance with the provisions of the lisbon treaty. the commission attaches great importance to simplifying and clarifying the law of the union so as to make it clearer and more accessible and understandable to citizens. for this reason the codification of rules that have frequently been amended is essential to make the law clearer and more transparent. the agreement reached in february is a step forward in this direction. i would like to thank the rapporteur once again.
on behalf of the ecr club. madam president, i congratulate rapporteur kuhn and the shadow rapporteurs on a quality report that addresses certain legislative problems that have for many years been an administrative obstacle to citizens exercising their rights. unfortunately, in this house and in national and regional parliaments, there is a clear trend towards complicated regulations written by experts for experts, and the common man in this jungle of rules and norms often does not get along. this report is the complete opposite and therefore a step in the right direction. the regulatory framework must be as simple and as applicable as possible in order to achieve the desired effects. it must be clear to those to whom it applies, not just to experts. the initiative to codify the 1986 regulation on the characteristics of fishing vessels is a good thing, because the coherent implementation of the common fisheries policy implies the same rules and definitions. but as i have said many times in this house, the common fisheries policy must take account of regional and local specificities and the socioeconomic and cultural importance of fisheries in individual regions. i am therefore particularly pleased that the codification process will take into account, in addition to the technical characteristics of the vessels themselves, their social role in coastal communities and their environmental impact and fishing capacity. i consider this proposal a major step in the right direction and will therefore support it in the vote.
the reporter. madam president, women living in rural areas deserve our full attention and our full engagement. their problems are real, and to understand them, we need to understand the diverse roles of women in rural areas. they take care of their families, they preserve tradition and the environment, they work in agriculture, which is one of the most important economic sectors of any country, they participate in the food production process. women in rural areas are invaluable to their family farms, and are important for the development of entrepreneurship, sustainable development and overall economic development. but they're not a homogeneous group, so i advocate a specific approach to their diverse roles. women in rural areas do not have good access to education, they are poorly computer literate, and internet access is still at a very low level. also, because of the distance between specialist doctors and the lack of diagnostic facilities, in most cases women do not go to a doctor until the disease is advanced, which is unfortunately sometimes too late for them. in rural areas, there is often no adequate social, social and communal infrastructure necessary for a decent life. women make up less than 50% of the total working population in rural areas of the european union and manage only 30% of family farms. during 2014, women accounted for around 35% of total working time in agriculture, making a significant contribution to agricultural production, and in addition, on farms, they take care of the family and household, often characterised as 'invisible' work. rural women are often economically dependent on their husbands, have no working hours, and struggle with the lack of basic social and pension rights such as substantially lower pensions or unpaid maternity leave. almost half of them never take a holiday and are very little involved in decision-making processes at local and regional level. no eu member state has yet established a thematic sub-programme for women in rural areas such as the one for young farmers, although this is provided for by the european agricultural fund for rural development, which is a clear indication that women in rural areas are simply not valued enough. this report recognises the invaluable contribution women make to the economy of rural areas and encourages member states to make greater and more synergistic use of the instruments available under the european agricultural fund for rural development, the leader+ programme and the european social fund. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the union's policy on the environment and its implementation in the member states. i hope that this report will help to raise awareness of the problems faced by women in rural areas and, in this sense, be a step towards their solution. the women of the village are the real heroes and that's why i'm dedicating this report to them.

on behalf of the s&d club. mr. president, medical devices are not important until they are needed, but the moment they are needed, they become the most important thing in the world. the main objective we want to achieve with these regulations is to better protect patients while encouraging innovation in industry. europe has the best doctors in the world, but they and their patients need to know that they have quality and safe equipment. trust in the industry is capitalized through better product placement and sales, so the new rules will actually benefit everyone. the commission published its initial proposal back in 2012, which means that european patients have been waiting for 5 years to close the loopholes in regulations that have caused scandals with artificial hips, implants and hiv testing. when a scandal happens in this sector, people die. that is why we insisted that new and innovative in vitro medical devices of the highest risk class for which there are no references and no common specifications be covered by an additional surveillance procedure. the new rules strengthen the capacities and structures of the designated bodies which have the most important role in placing devices on the market. their responsibilities and obligations are increasing, and one of the novelties is unannounced inspections of manufacturers. when scandals occur and patients suffer, the issue of fair and timely compensation becomes imperative. new regulations require medical device manufacturers to have an adequate system and resources in place to compensate patients in the event of an incident. in addition, manufacturers will have to provide injured parties and competent services with appropriate access to information and documentation related to their case. as far as the ethics committee and issues like clinical trials of devices on minors are concerned, from day one we have insisted that the regulations be aligned with the regulations on drug trials. i think we've managed to strike a good compromise that raises the level of safety for patients and users. the new procedures and safety mechanisms will not be an obstacle to the innovation and competitiveness of the european medical device industry. so i urge you all to support this compromise, because it's in the best interest of both patients and medical device manufacturers. in closing, i'd like to thank my colleague glenis willmott, my colleague peter liese and all the other shadow reporters i've worked with on this documentary. it has been a very difficult and painstaking job, but the results will be a joy to all of us, especially the patients.
mr president, commissioner mimica, i congratulate my fellow rapporteurs on a very well balanced report which calls for a common approach and response to the unprecedented wave of migration facing the european union. i believe that the three key messages that we need to send through this report, both to our citizens and to our global partners, are: the only lasting solution to the migration crisis is at its source, the european union's external policy must take a more assertive role in reconciliation processes to stem new refugee flows, and the european union must take the lead in coordinating the instruments for funding migration management together with other international institutions and communicate more visibly its efforts to help eradicate poverty and intolerance, the main causes of injustice that force people to leave their homes. we must not forget countries with fragile inter-confessional populations like lebanon, which in these difficult times are dealing with the enormous pressure of refugees, who make up half of their population. they urgently need help to prevent the dam from overflowing and europe from facing even greater problems.
mr. president, congratulations to the reporters on a worthy job. i welcome the initiative to help the union's southern neighbourhood and the western balkans to create jobs, economic resilience and reduce poverty, and in particular the emphasis on the need for a decentralised approach in the conduct of european migration policy. however, key questions remain unanswered. who caused the migrant crisis? how much responsibility does the european union have? and how should the eu act preventively? the european union must strictly control the flow of migrants and carry out systematic surveillance of its external borders. the european union must of course show solidarity and help vulnerable groups, especially women and children, but it must not ignore the issue of security and the fact that a large number of refugees are able-bodied men. solidarity and humanity must not be used as a pretext for a cultural change in europe, and the european union must not become a refugee camp or a final destination for refugees.
mr president, the migration crisis, which is still not over, represents the biggest migration challenge europe has faced since the second world war. we cannot remain indifferent to the suffering of people coming from regions affected by the raging war, but we cannot continue with the naive and disastrous policy of accepting everyone who shows up at our borders. protecting a life-threatening victim is one thing, and having no criteria is another. brussels' solutions have proven bad, for migrants as well as for the host countries. migrants were sent under quotas to countries that they didn't want to go to and that for various reasons didn't want to accept them. it makes no sense because it violates the will of both the member states and the migrants. no one should have the right to decide for a member state what its society will look like, who it will accept and how it will integrate these people, as long as the state respects the fundamental values and the legal acquis of the european union.
written. people migrate and leave their homes every day for a variety of reasons, voluntarily and involuntarily. today, there are some 244 million international migrants, and this indicates that migration has reached unprecedented proportions. the alarming fact is that in the last five years, the number of refugees has increased by as much as 50 percent. the most vulnerable are the minors on the mediterranean migration stream, who cross borders in the hope of a better future, risking their lives, because despite the rescue efforts, the death toll is constantly rising. they are exposed to a range of psychological and physical dangers, including various forms of violence, and it is alarming that women and children, who are the least vulnerable to these dangers, are a particularly vulnerable group. i believe that, aware of the fact that international migration can contribute to socioeconomic development, the international community must representatively highlight the benefits of migration and urgently take measures to strengthen the collective response in order to protect the rights and dignity of persons who have been forced to leave their homes. only a common struggle can put an end to xenophobic, populist and nationalist discourse.

madam president, social media is a revolution in human communication and a huge boost to the global economy. thanks to them, information is being disseminated globally in seconds, and companies are getting to their customers more cheaply than ever before. but like any revolution, social media is often abused, and the latest trend of fake news is actually the least of our worries. our priority must be to stop the growing trend of using social networks for serious crime such as pedophilia, prostitution, human trafficking, terrorist recruitment and similar illegal activities. let's not forget that the mainstream media, knowingly or unknowingly, has also been very much about publishing inaccuracies, so any form of social media censorship would automatically introduce self-censorship in the media, because they would be afraid to publish intriguing information. i'm not for restricting free speech, but i am for taking full responsibility for malicious information that can cause people psychological or material harm.
written. the internet can be a very useful tool for achieving freedom of expression, although it is also necessary to take into account the harmful purposes it can have. freedom of expression is one of the fundamental rules of the european union, but the increasing number of fake social media profiles indicates that they are being used to spread hate speech, fake news, recruitment and propaganda, thus violating fundamental human rights. extremism is increasingly present on social networks, and consequently some member states are introducing specific laws to prevent it. clearly, clear guidelines are needed to take effective measures to limit the spread of hate speech and fake news.

the reporter. madam president, the european union, as one of the most advanced and wealthy communities in the world, has a moral and political obligation to reduce the enormous amounts of food that is thrown away each year. as long as there are public kitchens and social services in our cities and one kilogram of discarded vegetables is too much. as long as our fellow citizens dig through the bins because they're hungry and one loaf of bread thrown away is too much. as long as children are starving in yemen and somalia and one bag of flour thrown away is too much. 55 million europeans cannot afford a quality meal at least every other day. about 88 million tons of food are thrown away every year. a situation where 20 percent of the food produced is not eaten or thrown away is unsustainable and immoral. the aim of this report is to propose to the european commission and the member states policy and practical measures to reduce food waste along the whole chain from production to sale to consumption. maintaining food safety standards is our red line because public health is our priority. the report calls on member states to take the necessary measures to reduce the union's food waste target by 30% by 2025 and by 50% by 2030. to achieve this, we need official definitions of food waste and food loss and a common methodology for measuring these phenomena. the commission should also be able to take into account the impact of the proposed legislation on the environment, in particular the impact of the new legislation on the environment. education is the key to reducing food waste. it is estimated that more than half of all food waste comes from households. the report calls on the commission, member states, local authorities and other stakeholders to carry out educational campaigns to inform the public about the value of food and the prevention of food waste. an example of misunderstanding the difference between "use to" and "best use to". less than half of the citizens actually know that there is a difference and that food can still be consumed after the expiry date of "best before". the commission is requested to review the appropriateness of the current labelling system and to propose changes if necessary. in some member states, vat is still charged on the donation of food, which is actually a direct consequence of european legislation. some countries have bypassed these rules, but we believe that the best solution would be for the commission to propose an amendment to the vat directive explicitly granting exemptions from payment of tax on food donations. at the same time, some member states encourage donation through tax breaks and other economic incentives for socially responsible companies. we believe that these and other examples of good practice should be promoted throughout the european union. eu funds and programmes such as the fund for aid to the most deprived and the agricultural fund for rural development have the potential to increase donations and reduce food losses. we consider that these opportunities are under-utilized and call on member states to become more involved in this issue. citizens' awareness of this problem is at a very high level today and we must not miss the opportunity to act. commissioners, member states, parliament calls for a reduction in food waste in the european union, because allow me, to conclude this important debate we have had this evening, not the only time we have had it, to speak in my own language, which is also one of the great languages of the christian community in the world. i don't think latin is included in the translations, so i'll stick with italian. as someone who was born and raised in rome, just a few hundred metres from the vatican, i think you can understand very well the importance of this debate to me tonight and i think you can have full confidence in the determination, commitment and energy that i personally put into the work we have talked about tonight. i would like to end this debate by making just three points. first, the european union has and will maintain human rights, all human rights, at the heart of its foreign policy. it is not the same for all international actors, at this particular moment, but for us it is a beacon of our action in the world. second, we believe that freedom of religion and worship is a fundamental right that must be guaranteed, protected and promoted for all religions and in all areas of the world. this is true for christians in the middle east, this is also true for christians elsewhere in the world, some of them rightly recall the persecutions and attacks on christian communities in other parts of the world, which are equally worrying, linked to different kinds of dynamics, but i would also like to recall, as some colleagues have done this evening, the need to protect all communities and all religions in our europe too. i would like to support those who have stressed the need to prevent and combat islamophobia, particularly in europe, quoting pope francis, who reminded us that europe has the duty to rediscover its capacity to integrate and to always recall, alongside europe's christian roots, the fact that we have other roots and that some can always live alongside others, in respect, tolerance and mutual protection. third point: the situation of christians in the middle east is not just a question of human rights, it is not just a question of tolerance, it is a question first of all of preserving diversity, the diversity of a region, the diversity of a culture, the diversity of a history. i agree with what some of my colleagues said during the debate, that this is a matter of policy and a matter of security, because the disappearance of christian communities from the middle east would change the very nature of middle eastern societies, with a very worrying consequence both from the political and the security point of view. that is why the european union religion anywhere in the world. in conclusion, i would like to thank parliament again for the debate and also many member states who are taking initiatives in this direction. some spanish colleagues on behalf of the alde club. madam president, commissioner, i ask you to listen carefully because i may be the main culprit in bringing this item back on the agenda today. on friday, april 7th, when the decree came into force, i was returning home from the last strasbourg session and at the border crossing of castel-dragon i was greeted by a two-kilometer column in which i waited for two hours. on saturday morning, i immediately wrote a letter to president juncker and prime minister plenkovic, who did call me after an hour or two and promised to arrange a meeting with prime minister cerar and president juncker. that meeting did indeed take place on april 29, and the result of their conversation was that if the queues were longer than 15 minutes, they would switch from total to targeted checks. the three friends' intentions are good, but they're more about appeasing the citizens' anger than solving the problem of unnecessary gridlock. in practice, this is, after all, subject to the discretion of individuals and is not formalised by any regulation whatsoever. the correct solution is to introduce targeted controls as a rule, as a written rule, especially at border crossings where the security risk is minimal, and to introduce total controls as an exception only in the case of risky intelligence. this regulation 458 does not objectively solve the problem of citizens' security, but it significantly complicates the life of those people who live near the border. i therefore ask the commission to propose a revision of the regulation immediately and to take into account the fact that the targeted controls that have been in practice until recently have proved to be an appropriate solution. and three more absurd. on 15 may, the commission lowered its gdp growth forecast for croatia due to 'unusually long queues at border crossings' as they directly harm tourism, which accounts for 20% of gdp. so the commission proposes regulations on the one hand and then gives a the reporter. mr president, ms crețu, cohesion policy is perhaps one of the best examples of how membership of the european union can be a positive thing for less developed member states. the single market and the four fundamental freedoms sound great in theory, but if the member states are too different in their level of economic development, it always seems that someone is taking advantage of someone else. from the less developed member countries, workers are leaving for the more developed ones willing to work for less money than the home-based labor force, which lowers the cost of labor and creates discontent among the local people. on the other hand, the less developed members are losing their working population and thus are entering even deeper into a long-term economic and demographic crisis. reducing these disparities so that they do not encourage excessive waves of migration from the poorer to the richer member states of the union and create discontent among citizens is very important for the future of the european union. cohesion policy has an important role to play in this process, and technical assistance, which we are talking about today, is a quality mechanism for creating the conditions for the good use of funds and the financing of quality projects in the less developed member states. the lack of administrative capacity in less developed member states is a major obstacle to better use of european money. post-communist countries tend to have large administrations in proportion to their population, but they are not sufficiently trained to draw funds from european funds quickly, efficiently and transparently. the changes that are certain to follow in cohesion policy after 2020 could leave the less developed member states unprepared, leaving them permanently unable to catch up with the developed ones. that is why the correct, efficient and transparent use of technical assistance in the next financial period is so important! member of the commission. – mr president, honourable members, dear rapporteur, i am glad to speak on behalf of commissioner věra jourová, my colleague, on this very important and so sensitive issue, as you rightly raised. it is very important to discuss it. existing eu legislation aims to ensure a high level of consumer protection, safety, and correct and reliable consumer information. we think that only safe food products are allowed on the eu market – as we said, we have a single market – and of course, consumers must be accurately informed and not misled about the composition and characteristics of food offered for sale. we have general labelling rules and requirements to that effect, and it is eu legislation which ensures that producers have to make a complete list of ingredients that enables the consumers to be fully informed of the composition of the food product. this being said, as long as products comply with eu legal requirements and their labelling does not mislead consumers as to the main characteristics, companies are not prevented from differentiating products according to the market. there is no legal basis, unfortunately, at eu level to challenge differences in food composition in individual member states, as long as the relevant food information is not misleading. however, should a trader advertise a product as being the same in several member states, but at the same time deliberately reduce the quality of the product in some of these countries compared to others, this practice could be addressed under the directive on unfair commercial practices by the competent national authority. eu consumer law allows national enforcement authorities or courts to end specific misleading commercial practices if they consider that they are harming consumers. after the investigations and many events coming from this house, the commission has called on member states to provide further data and evidence in order to assess the existence and extent of these alleged practices and possible reasons for them. so far, the commission has received 21 replies from the member states. according to the received replies, the issue of dual quality is considered a big problem, especially in the czech republic, hungary, slovakia and croatia. the main products concern food, laundry detergents and pet food. the investigations carried out in several countries showed said the labelling of these products corresponds to their composition. in other member states there is no particular data or evidence related to the issue of dual quality so far. in order to tackle this important issue, more cooperation among enforcement agencies is needed, so as to elaborate an objective mapping and to compare products sold in several member states. the commission intends to provide the results of this mapping for the next sherpa meeting of the high-level forum for a better functioning of the food supply chain on 20 june, in line with a conclusion of the european council of 9 march 2017. this forum will enable, for the first time, an exchange of views between all concerned actors, such as consumer representatives, industry at various stages of the supply chain, member states’ authorities and the commission. some of you have called on the commission to propose a specific legislation to prohibit dual quality. in our view, the reinforcement of existing laws should be strengthened in the first place. i trust that the on-going debate on the process of collecting evidence will raise the awareness of all concerned players on the issue and help address it to the benefit of all european citizens and consumers. on our part, we will build on the results of the consultation of national authorities and the outcome of debate in the high-level forum. i am looking forward for our debate.

written. in today's global competition for new technological advances such as 5g networks, cloud computing, artificial intelligence and robotics, the question we ask is how to integrate them into business models, value chains, production and consumption in the most sustainable and socially acceptable way? the eu must respond to this by sustainably innovating and adapting its education system to remain competitive and become a leader in the new industrial revolution. in this area, the eu's strengths are its specific know-how, its linked and targeted r & d investment, its large single market, its strong industrial base and its experience in linking production and services. the key to progress is the education of workers and the acquisition of it skills, because without people with the necessary skills, there is no significant shift in the digitisation of industry. digitalisation also means the need for different jobs and new skills. only with a well-adapted education and science system can we prepare our citizens for the digital age that is no longer the future. combining digital skills with vocational education and engineering skills, smart specialisation, connecting smes, industrial entities, start-ups, scientists and researchers is the right way to adapt to change. the commission should also be able to assess the impact of the proposed measures on the digital divide in the union.

mr president, in recent years, counterfeit visa stickers have appeared in several member states. according to experts, the current label is no longer safe. this is not surprising given that the current security concept of the uniform visa form dates back to 1995 when it was developed within the framework of interstate schengen cooperation. everything has been modified twice, and the safety circumstances and technological standards have changed completely since then. improving entry controls into the european union is becoming dramatically linked to the issue of protecting citizens. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environmentally friendly use of energy. that is why i support the development of a new visa sticker with advanced security features to prevent counterfeiting and to make illegal immigration more difficult. new security threats are increasingly a challenge not only to national but also to european and global institutions. they will define the character of the union in the first decades of this century. they are already increasingly shaping citizens' awareness of the need for closer security sector cooperation between member states. the european union must finally confront the deterioration of security conditions within and beyond its borders, first by helping member states to cooperate and exchange information better, and second by stabilising conflicting political communities in the neighbourhood. security must not remain the weakest link in european integration, and this proposal is a step in the right direction.
mr president, the elderly who are temporarily or permanently incapable of taking care of themselves are one of the particularly vulnerable groups towards which any mature society feels a special obligation. we often talk in this home about the positive rights of the various social groups that make up healthy adults, capable of work and functioning unhindered in society. i'm glad to discuss today also those for whom our solidarity is a basic prerequisite for a normal life. the protection of vulnerable adults in the exercise of their freedom of movement is a particular challenge due to the still existing fragmentation of justice systems in europe. the promotion of full recognition of protection measures adopted by the competent authorities of one member state in all other member states and the sharing of information on the protection status are key recommendations of this report which i can only welcome. their full implementation will provide vulnerable adults with a higher level of legal certainty and is therefore a step in the right direction.

madam president, the challenges that the developing countries faced 20 years ago are now, after many years of prosperity, also relevant in our european countries. poverty, insecurity and social exclusion are commonplace for millions of europeans. i will always support the constructive role of the european union in the poorest parts of the world and all positive efforts to get them back on their feet, free of imperialist ambitions and exploitative intentions, so that we can trade on an equal footing and prosper together. but i am of the opinion that the union's role in the world must not eclipse the role it must play at home. the eu must encourage member states to make it easier for people to do business and get a job, and to invest in infrastructure so that all parts of europe are well connected and live in a way that is fit for the 21st century.
mr president, investing in research, development and innovation is investing in the future of our children. thanks to technological advances, the world is rapidly moving forward, old status and wealth are becoming less important, and new markets are growing and displacing old ones. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to improve the quality of life in the regions. i support horizon 2020 because i believe that research and innovation are areas where public investment is necessary and justified. however, we must also work to attract private investment and i am so glad that the rapporteur has recognised this. but i cannot agree with the recommendation that we give priority to funding climate change research as the united states is considering budget cuts in this area. our strategy for investing in r&d and innovation must be based on our needs and long-term goals, not on the day-to-day politics of other global actors.
mr president, the basic idea of the european union is open political cooperation and free economic exchange between countries that are naturally drawn to each other, share the same values and want to achieve the same goals in the future. for this to be possible, european nation states must have roughly similar starting positions, because otherwise old animosities and the feeling that there are those who profit and those who are exploited in this relationship will be reawakened. if we get to that point, the idea of european integration will be seriously compromised. the key role of cohesion policy is precisely to ensure a similar starting position for all, despite the different historical circumstances in which the current member states have developed. the aim is to create a europe of equal opportunities based on the principle of solidarity, and we must not give up on this. cohesion policy must be preserved despite political crises such as brexit, it must be free of administrative barriers and simplified to the maximum. we need to integrate funds from different funds and prepare programmes in advance at regional and member state level to avoid a repeat of the bad experience of the previous financial period. i also support the strengthening of administrative capacity, but i stress that this should not mean an increase in the size of the administration, but an increase in its efficiency. we must avoid further bureaucratization at all costs!
the situation of fish stocks in the mediterranean is not good, mr president, and this has been fostered by the policies which have been pursued in the past, which have taken a one-dimensional view of fisheries. the shift that is already taking place in fisheries policy must be based on scientific research, concrete data and an open dialogue with all fishermen and their representatives. last year, this house overwhelmingly approved my report on innovation and diversification of small-scale coastal fisheries in fisheries-dependent areas. but the commission is still deaf to this house's call for the amendment of the mediterranean regulation, whose full implementation has significantly damaged the fisheries sector in croatia and pushed thousands of families into a difficult existence. we cannot allow fishermen to blame each other for this situation because it takes us away from the solution. i therefore join the rapporteur, mr affronte, in calling for a clear definition of certain types of fishing and, on the basis of scientific research, for an assessment of their impact on the state of fish stocks and the environment in general. only then can we devise and implement good policies that balance the interests of all fishermen and, of course, the conservation of marine resources. i congratulate my colleague, mr affronta, on a brilliant report which i will of course support in the vote.
written. the mediterranean sea is one of the most biodiverse areas in the world, and has a significant socioeconomic impact on coastal populations and directly contributes to food security. in addition, a large number of local communities depend on small-scale, traditional, artisanal and recreational fisheries, which represent a major socio-economic and tourist value and have a significant impact on employment. however, the mediterranean also faces serious problems of overfishing, increasing maritime traffic, densely populated coasts and increasing pollution that require a joint response based on cooperation at all levels, from international to local. i believe that special attention should be paid to the restoration and maintenance of fish stocks in order to preserve the biodiversity and natural wealth of the mediterranean sea. in addition, it is necessary to apply environmental, economic and social criteria, respecting the precautionary approach in terms of the protection of cultural, historical and traditional heritage, socio-economic circumstances, while preventing unreported, illegal and irregular fishing. i believe that the effective implementation of the guidelines for the sustainable and responsible management of marine resources requires strong comprehensive measures and policies. the commission has also proposed that the member states should be able to provide for the establishment of a european fisheries fund to support the fisheries sector in the mediterranean. of course, this must be accompanied by the collection and sharing of scientific data, and a high level of accountability and awareness.
written. the gender pension gap in many eu member states is a matter of serious concern. as part of a comprehensive strategy to fight for gender equality in all spheres of life, the harmonisation of pension systems is of particular importance for all members of society who are at higher risk of poverty due to the systematic restriction of their rights. the gap in average pre-tax income received as a pension in 2014 was 39,4% in the 65+ age group and we note a dramatic increase in these figures in more than half of the member states in the last five years. the lower pensions of women are a direct consequence of the gender pay gap, which, despite union equality legislation, is still widespread in the member states of the european union. i support this bill because of the consistent and urgent need to change the gender pension gap. the european union cannot tolerate systematic discrimination against women. pensions are the main source of income for one in four eu citizens. the dramatic situation of the pensioner will worsen if we do not first raise public awareness of the existing shortcomings and then take all necessary measures to remove the existing obstacles.

mr. president, stateless people around the world are condemned to a life of utter disenfranchisement, often on the margins of society. they cannot obtain a qualification, have health insurance, legally work, marry, or enjoy a host of other civil and political rights. the situation of persons in many countries in south and south-east asia is of particular concern, and our knowledge of the number of persons who are not citizens of the country where they were born and spent their lives is limited, given that such data are very poorly collected or published in some countries in the region. while the current situation of stateless people can be attributed to inefficient administration, serious problems arise where a group of people is marginalized for political purposes. the systematic discrimination against minorities, such as in myanmar, constitutes a serious violation of human rights and the union must do everything to improve the situation of vulnerable groups in the region, including the recognition of these persons as citizens of their home country. i support the motion because of the crucial importance of the issue of stateless people. we need to clearly identify where there is systemic discrimination and where there is a lack of administrative capacity and, depending on the circumstances, take the necessary measures.

the commission has also proposed that the european investment bank (eib) should be established as a new entity, with a mandate to provide financial support to the eib in the form of grants, loans and guarantees. i must admit that from day one it was not clear to me how the commission intends to ensure that public investment reinforces the effect of private investment if it has not first ensured a more favourable climate for private investment. as is usually the case when a large injection of taxpayers' money into the economy is planned, many projects could be financed without union guarantees and the decision-making process was rather opaque. the geographical spread of investments is also a concern, as by the end of june 2016, as much as 91% of the funds were allocated to the eu-15 countries. such things undermine the credibility of the union and its programmes because they give the impression that some members are profiting at the expense of others. if we are to avoid such interpretations and raise the profile of the european union, the implementation of the european fund for strategic investments must be much better.
madam president, the efsi is meeting its objective, which is to raise the level of investment. however, we want more than that. we want growth, development and high growth and development rates in the european union and new jobs. for that we need the other two pillars of the investment plan for europe. the first pillar is to identify good and quality projects that are innovative and risky. do we currently finance such projects and have we found the right ones for efsi funding? and the other pillar that still remains is strengthening the single market and creating a favourable environment for our entrepreneurs and investors. the european commission cannot do this alone. this requires coordination and work with member states, and i therefore call on the commission to strengthen its links and links with member states to make the investment plan for europe a reality.
madam president, children are one of those vulnerable groups that a mature society must take special care of. according to eurostat data from december 2016, one in four children is at risk of poverty or social exclusion. the child poverty survey and the strategy for coping with household poverty in croatia show that the relative poverty rate of children in our country is about 7% higher than the european union average. the data are alarming, but we must not let them guide us only to the adoption of new expensive social policies, but we must primarily take care to create the conditions for employment and the creation of well-paid jobs, in order to achieve greater employment of parents and increase their wages. this is impossible with the high tax burden needed to fund expensive social policies, and we must be careful not to fall into that trap. children can live with dignity only if their parents earn a decent income from their work.

member of the commission. – madam president, the commission shares broadly the problem analysed and presented by the honourable member. one of the main objectives of the european union is to protect vulnerable groups, including minors, consumers and citizens in general through harmonisation of laws. the commission is fully committed both to safeguarding freedom of expression and protecting vulnerable viewers – children in particular – from harmful content, and all citizens against hate speech. article 14 of the e-commerce directive needs to be interpreted on a case—by—case basis. while the e-commerce directive prohibits member states from imposing a general monitoring obligation on providers, it requires providers to act upon obtaining actual knowledge of illegal activity in order to avoid liability. the commission believes that progress can be made without reopening the e-commerce directive, which has been a major contributor to innovation and societal benefits. the european union has proposed targeted measures to ensure that platforms take action against specific problems, such as illegal hate speech and terrorist propaganda. the commission has also initiated and strongly supported co-regulatory efforts. the commission communication on the mid-term review of the digital single market of 10 may 2017 announced a better coordination of dialogues with platforms, focusing on the mechanisms and technical solutions for the removal of illegal content. this communication also announced the commission’s guidelines on two topics: first, coherent procedural aspects such as the notification and removal of illegal content; second, on liability rules and support to platforms on voluntary measures taken by the platform when they work proactively to remove illegal content, acting in good faith. the commission is currently reflecting on the most appropriate form for such guidance. the issue of protection of minors and hate speech on video-sharing platforms is addressed by the proposals of a review of the audiovisual media service directive adopted on 25 may 2016, which is currently under discussion by the council and parliament. the commission proposal foresees a limited extension of its scope to video-sharing platforms, for example youtube. the proposed system seeks to keep the balance between the fundamental right of freedom of expression and important public interest objectives, such as the protection of minors against harmful content and the protection of all citizens against hate speech. according to the proposals, only those rules that are necessary to protect the viewers from the most harmful content will apply to video-sharing platforms. member states should ensure that video-sharing platforms put in place measures to 1) protect minors from harmful content, access to which would have to be restricted, and 2) protect all citizens from incitement to hate. the measures consist, where appropriate, in establishing and operating flagging and reporting mechanisms, age verification systems, systems to read the content by the uploaders or users, or parental control systems. it is important to stress that none of the measures included in the proposal entail a general obligation to monitor or ex—ante control; it is fully in line with the e-commerce directive. member states are encouraged to implement the new rules via co-regulation. the involvement of the industry will ensure that measures are future-proofed and that platform is built on what they are already doing on a voluntary basis. some platforms are indeed already making great efforts to protect minors online and citizens from hate speech. through the implementation via co-regulation, we are aiming to a building on the work already done. however, there are services that do not have such measures in place. now all video-sharing platforms will be obliged to take measures. the audiovisual media service directive would provide regulators with a regulatory backstop in case there is no action from platforms. in addition, video-sharing platforms today apply their own standards when it comes to assessing what is harmful for minors or what constitutes hate speech. the commission proposal aims that they have to comply with the audiovisual directive standards in this field. the commission believes that the proposed rules will ensure a better protection of viewers in online services. the commission is also looking forward to the upcoming trialogues, and hopes to secure the adoption of the revised audiovisual media service directive as soon as possible, as this is a priority file and identified by the institutions.
on behalf of the alde club. madam president, first of all i would like to thank my colleague malteze for raising this important issue. under the current rules, that is, this directive in question, service providers are not obliged to monitor the content they post, and they are not liable if they have no knowledge that some content is illegal. at the same time, member states can get information on the service providers, so identify who it is, but this indirectly means their obligation to monitor the content, which actually says that there is a high responsibility of the member states and probably the impossibility of monitoring this whole process, and a minimal responsibility of the service provider. this is certainly not a good situation and i therefore welcome the commission's willingness to work to protect all citizens, and in particular the most vulnerable, namely children and the elderly, when amending this and other similar directives.
