madam president, europe has one of the highest standards of health care in the world, and treatment is generally available to all citizens regardless of their financial status or any other economic or demographic characteristics. nevertheless, in recent years europe has become increasingly aware of the importance of prevention. the increase in the number of people with aids, tuberculosis, and hepatitis c that we have seen in recent years is a sign that we need to do more to raise awareness among citizens and strengthen public health policies. our efforts should not be confined to the member states alone, but we must also cooperate as much as possible in this area with neighbouring countries and with those third countries from which the largest number of people migrate to europe.
madam president, many of us have experienced that our device "crkne" immediately after the expiration of the warranty. it can't be a coincidence and it has to be banned. as many as 92 percent of french citizens believe that the products they buy are programmed to expire. in addition, for example, when the washing machine bearing cracks, you have to replace the entire drum because there are no smaller spare parts. sometimes the spare parts are so expensive that they don't even pay to fix them. in germany alone, the number of repair shops fell by 13 percent in one year. it is necessary to ensure the availability and reasonable cost of spare parts in the event of minor equipment failures. it has been demonstrated that the introduction of a minimum durability marking would lead to an increase in sales of the labelled products by 55%. consumers are willing to pay more for product durability. i think this is a problem that needs to be addressed at both the national and european level. studies have shown that reuse or repair is even better than recycling, because it creates over 10,000 new jobs and has a better impact on the environment.
written. the fundamental values on which the european union is founded are respect for human dignity, freedom, democracy, equality, the rule of law and respect for human rights. adherence to these fundamental values lies at the heart of the great peace project we now call the european union. the european union must actively and consistently promote its values not only towards its member states but also in the world. i support the motion for measures to protect human rights and prevent the most serious crimes. the union must demonstrate stronger support for human rights as enshrined in international law in its external action towards third countries. the problem of impunity for perpetrators of serious crimes, including those who commit crimes on behalf of non-state actors, can only be overcome by the comprehensive jurisdiction of the international criminal court. i deplore the widespread disregard for international humanitarian law and the alarming rates of civilian deaths and attacks on civilian infrastructure in armed conflicts around the world. the eu must work closely with all relevant united nations bodies with relevant information to make the prevention of mass crime as effective and timely as possible. i welcome the use of european instruments to promote the principle of complementarity in order to also provide for the prosecution of the most serious crimes in international criminal law in national legislation.
mr. president, being employed is better than being unemployed. let's get on with it. part-time or temporary jobs are better than long-term unemployment. this does not mean, of course, that such jobs should be pursued, but neither should they be avoided forever because, as this report repeatedly points out, they are "unsafe". the politics here then try to present themselves as protectors of the people and guarantee that they will make these jobs safe. the only job that's safe in the long run is the one that's commercially viable. any business that is not commercially viable, and that survives with the help of politics, does so at the expense of another that then also becomes unsustainable. the more policy saves, the more unsustainable it is, which then in times of recession completely sinks and causes a chain reaction. if we are to understand each other, we must strive to make working conditions as good as possible for all europeans. but i fear that we will not achieve this by additional regulation, but solely by encouraging competition and removing barriers from the single market.

mr president, the european parliament's structure in three different locations, in brussels, strasbourg and luxembourg, is expensive, inefficient, harmful to the environment and a great relic of a bygone era. we have finally had an official debate on this problem and i can only welcome that. over the years, a whole network of political and economic actors has been created around each of the parliament's seats, benefiting from this type of structure, so that a completely logical rationalisation in the form of relocating the entire infrastructure to one place was practically impossible to achieve. i believe that we will succeed in the end, despite the enormous resistance. strasbourg as a place of reconciliation has great symbolic value, but i think it is time that parliament stopped paying the price of symbolism. moving to one place will save us hundreds of millions of euros and thousands of man-hours spent on trips to strasbourg and will make us much more productive in our work. i think we owe it all to the taxpayers.
mr president, the outermost regions, because of their geographical position, face challenges that do not afflict much of continental europe. although these are territories that are part of the european union, their isolation and geographical distance from the political centre of the union, brussels, affects their consumption of european laws and policies. this must change and the outermost regions must become an equal part of the union in every respect. although their problems are specific, they are unfortunately not alone in their traffic isolation and poor use of cohesion policy instruments. i'll give the example of the croatian islands because i live on one of them myself. the demographic and economic decline of the islands on the croatian side of the adriatic also requires an urgent response. through simplified procedures for the use of european funds and new policies for island development, we can reverse this process and help islands, as well as the outermost regions, to significantly contribute to economic development and the preservation of local communities.

participation in horizon 2020 with regard to the trans—european networks in the field of transport. for instance, the report calls on the commission to ensure the effective integration of these regions. the commission will explore how to include these regions better in the trans-european transport network, as connectivity remains an important challenge to address. the report also highlights the importance of specific regimes, such as specific taxation schemes. the commission is aware of the role these schemes play in terms of supporting the outermost regions in developing their economies. however, their evaluation and possible adaptation over time are needed. the report also acknowledges the importance of agriculture – as you have already said – and specific support for the outermost regions to address their particular agricultural challenges. without prejudice to the negotiation on the future eu multiannual financial framework (mff), the commission is committed to maintaining the specific aid provided by the programme of options specifically relating to remoteness and insularity (posei). regarding your concerns about the situation of the fishing fleet, we agree on the need for safer and more efficient vessels. however, the total fishing capacity should be commensurate with the sustainable exploitation of fisheries stocks. in order to achieve that, we need data on the state of the stocks, which we currently lack. to fill this information gap, we will need the active involvement of both the member states and their outermost regions so as to understand the situation of these stocks better and to recognise if management measures are needed. as for aid towards the construction of new vessels, something currently not eligible for public funding in the eu, the commission understands the concerns of the regions and is examining ways, within its competences, to address them while remaining consistent with its internal and international commitments. the commission will also look at the proposal for a dedicated support system for sustainable fisheries in these regions in the context of preparation for the post—2020 mff, following close consultation with stakeholders and on the basis of an appropriate impact assessment. with regard to international trade agreements, the commission will continue to pay attention to outermost regions’ and build local capacities. this external action has a direct impact on our internal security. nevertheless, such missions and operations can only take place outside the union, and that continues to be the case. irrespective of the particular circumstances of a conflict or crisis, the union’s action is always guided by respect for the principles of the united nations charter and international law, which are essential elements of our union. the eu response to external conflicts, crises and instability will always combine a number of instruments, ranging from diplomacy, development and other forms of assistance to civilian or military crisis management. the complex challenges we face today cannot be tackled by one instrument alone, be it military or other. the eu has a unique mix of tools military, state and non-state instruments.

member of the commission. – madam president, i warmly welcome the ambitious and well-balanced agreement reached between the european parliament, the council and the commission at the last trilogue in april on the review of the regulation on security of gas supply. it is a major political success on which we have worked hard together in record time. this is also thanks to you, mr buzek, and i congratulate you on your excellent work and thank you and all shadow-rapporteurs for your commitment in this file. the review of the regulation on security of gas supply is an important milestone in rolling out the energy union strategy. this agreement now completes the energy security package proposed by the commission just last year, in february 2016. one of the energy union objectives is to ensure secure, sustainable, competitive and affordable energy supplies for european consumers – for households and for businesses. europe’s energy market is changing rapidly and significantly. while the current regulation on security of gas supply of 2010 has already significantly improved member states’ preparedness for a gas disruption, it was clear from the stress tests conducted in 2014 that the instruments available under that regulation needed substantial improvements. the new regulation that is subject to a vote today brings a step change, enhancing and making operational the necessary instruments to prepare for and mitigate potential gas disruption: regional cooperation becomes real and operational; better transparency on gas supply contracts will help improve the risk assessment; the spirit of solidarity is for the first time translated into concrete action and cooperation between member states so that no household in any member state remains in the cold during a gas supply crisis. i am very happy that the most important elements of the commission proposals have been preserved or even strengthened during the negotiations. let me just highlight some of these elements, starting with regional cooperation. the current regulation includes only an optional cooperation between neighbours. in the new regulation, regional cooperation becomes mandatory and will take place in the risk groups created on the basis of the assessment of major transnational risks, drawing also on insights from european union-wide simulations done by entsog (the european network of transmission system operators for gas). the regional and national risk assessments will provide a sound basis for the national plans, with regional chapters containing meaningful cross-border measures. the spirit of solidarity is a cornerstone of the new regulation, ensuring that all households will receive gas supplies even during a crisis. with the new processes laid down in the regulation, for the first time, solidarity is not only a political declaration but an obligation and a concrete way to help. finally, transparency. information exchange and the transparency of gas supply contracts is also significantly strengthened and will allow for a more complete and in-depth assessment of risk to security of supply. first, the companies will provide contractual information to their national authorities. if the national authority has doubts about possible risks for security of supply related with the contract, it will notify the contract to the commission. the notification threshold of 28% for all gas supply contracts – existing and new – is well balanced. it is important to note that at any time the commission retains the power to ask for any contracts if it is concerned that a given contract is relevant for the assessment of the security of supply. and confidentiality will be guaranteed. the information on gas supply contracts will be sent to the commission in a non-aggregated but anonymised form. energy efficiency will be taken into account throughout the regulation, in particular when designing preventive measures. with the new regulation, cooperation on gas security of supply with the contracting parties of the energy community will become much closer and stronger. all the instruments provided through this new regulation will make the member states, and the european union as a whole, better prepared for a gas crisis and better equipped to mitigate a gas crisis should it arise. on this basis, i am confident that the vote in plenary today will give a strong backing to the important agreement that we have achieved together.
madam president, the gas supply crises that we have witnessed several times over the last 15 years or so suggest that the european union will have to change its energy policy, as well as its rules of conduct in crisis situations, if it is to achieve energy security and long-term stability. this proposal is an important step in the right direction. any type of crisis in the union causes the same pattern of behaviour in the member states, which is to shut down. in the event of a future energy crisis, it is important to ensure that cross-border gas distribution is not interrupted and this is addressed in a high-quality way by this proposal. the report strongly promotes the diversity of energy sources and renewable sources. i'd agree with that, but with the caveat that our energy goals have to be aligned with fiscal reality. we should invest most in those sources that offer the most value for money and create the least damage, and those are often not the sources that certain lobbies are pushing hard.
mr. president, first of all, i'd like to congratulate mr. buzek on his excellent work. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the energy union's energy efficiency targets and will continue to monitor the implementation of the energy union's energy efficiency targets and will continue to monitor the implementation of the energy union's energy efficiency targets. also, our esteemed commissioner cañete has been given a powerful tool, and that is to ensure that climate targets are also met in this way, because the natural gas that will be used in this way will help that transition by 2050 along our trajectories.

mr. president, fire safety is important to our entire society. statistics show that at least five thousand fires occur every day in the european union. that's two million fires a year, and 90 percent of them start in buildings. in recent decades, fires have grown faster than ever and have become more dangerous. this is largely due to the increased use of combustible materials in buildings. most european countries have not updated their fire regulations adequately to align with new building materials on the market. at eu level, fire-testing and classification standards are also not updated accordingly. we must insist on the highest level of fire protection for the citizens of the european union. anything less is not enough. we can and must make the european union fire-safe. we need to protect our citizens, but also the firefighters, whose health is also being compromised and who are also at greater risk of cancer. as a volunteer firefighter, i would like to take this opportunity to thank all the firefighters across the european union who save lives and property and who, by their example and their lives, are a testimony to the kind of people we should be.

written. being part of the erasmus+ programme is much more than just studying abroad. it is an experience that opens your eyes to new opportunities, friendships, knowledge and values. the programme, which started in 1987 as a voluntary cooperation between 11 european countries, has now developed into a single global network. the programme is celebrating its 30th anniversary in 2017 and over the past 30 years has undergone a radical transformation, leaving its mark on the personal and professional lives of some 9 000 000 students, trainees, volunteers, teachers, staff, educators and those working with young people. the erasmus+ programme is still only available to a very limited number of european citizens, but a further increase in the budget is expected by 2020. this unsatisfactory situation creates a sense of discontent because the programme does not meet the high expectations of eu citizens. the commission will adopt the necessary financial and budgetary reforms to achieve the ambitious objectives of this flagship eu programme of improving the conditions for everyone who decides to join it. i believe that the resolution on the future of erasmus+ helps eu citizens to find their place in our societies and to develop a sense of european identity - an identity that complements our national, regional and local identities. i see this resolution as a step forward in improving the functioning of the programme, with a view to making an even greater impact on the daily lives of eu citizens, and in particular young people.

weeks and we also have to look at the history of the country. in the last few years the country has gone through impressive change and yet the path towards an inclusive and diverse democracy is far from accomplished. let me start by stating clearly one thing – the european union has condemned the attacks of 25 august by the arakan rohingya salvation army, as well as the violence that followed in northern rakhine state. we believe that the perpetrators must be brought to justice in line with the rule of law. let me also state, just as clearly as what i said just now, that we are very much aware of and concerned about the excesses during the security operations conducted by myanmar security forces. this has led to a massive flow of refugees into bangladesh, one of the most terrible refugee crises of our time. this situation is completely unacceptable and risks destabilising the wider region. we have two immediate priorities and i was glad to see that the un security council discussed this issue last night. un secretary-general antónio guterres expressed similar priorities to the one i will share with you in a moment. first immediate priority: end the tensions, the violence; de—escalate. violence must stop immediately. human rights must be respected by all but it is very clear: security forces face a special responsibility to refrain from any violence against unarmed civilians. secondly, and i think mr preda mentioned this, humanitarian aid should be brought to those in need urgently. here we have a european union role to play that we are playing in a quite a remarkable manner. i would like here to thank commissioner stylianides for the dedication of his work, which is also proving to be essential in this moment and in this case. there are 350 000 vulnerable people in rakhine. they need to be urgently reached by humanitarian aid and aid workers. for many of them, this is a matter of life or death. my colleague stylianides and i are working very closely to address this issue and yesterday, upon our insistence, a lot of diplomatic work, the government communicated that the international red cross will be able to fly in and aid 30 000 of them. but let me be clear, this is a welcome step that can only be the beginning of a path that we have to see continuing, because the bangladeshi authorities have welcomed the many refugees who fled their homes and the international community – and we, first of all, i believe – should not only praise but also support their efforts. as an immediate response to the current situation, the european union has decided to increase our support to bangladesh with another eur 3 million, on top of the eur 12 million aid already provided for myanmar and bangladesh together. these are the two immediate priorities: end the violence and bring in humanitarian aid. but then we have another important task to carry out. we will continue to work with everyone involved to find lasting peace in myanmar to the benefit of the whole population, addressing the structural issue of rakhine. as you mentioned, this is not the first time we have come on this issue. this means that, beyond the acute moment of crisis, we have a responsibility to work on the political scene. there is no doubt that one of the underlying causes of instability in the region is the status of the rohingya minority in myanmar and the underdevelopment of rakhine state. addressing the situation is what we have always asked for in our political dialogue with myanmar over the years, including at high-level meetings and in the eu-myanmar human rights dialogue. as you know, the last resolution adopted by the human rights council, last march, was also a european union initiative. the recommendations of the final report of the advisory commission on rakhine state headed by kofi annan are our point of reference. the government of myanmar has committed itself to taking this report forward and i discussed this issue personally with aung san suu kyi during her visit to brussels just a few months ago. recommendations include the issue of discrimination, poverty alleviation, assurance of basic rights, equal access to education and social services, as well as the promotion of trust and reconciliation. the time to implement the annan report is now because we are seeing what the people of rakhine are facing and they cannot wait any longer. we are ready to support the implementation of these recommendations in all possible ways. the government of myanmar should also continue its cooperation with the united nations and make it stronger including with the un special rapporteur and the un human rights council’s independent fact-finding mission. this is also something we discussed with aung san suu kyi and just last week, once again, all these points were raised directly with the government of myanmar by a european union delegation headed by the european external action service (eeas) and, as you can imagine, by our eu delegation on a daily basis. our coordination stays strong with the un, personal contacts from my side with secretary-general guterres and with my colleagues all over the world on this issue, and we will use the un general assembly ministerial week for continuing intensive consultations and to guarantee that the international community can stay united in facing this issue. myanmar’s struggle for democracy has been a source of inspiration for many around the world, and for me personally and i believe for many of you in this room. the responsibility that the country’s leadership, civilian but also military, faces today is immense exactly because they have been, san suu kyi in particular, a source of inspiration for the democratic world. they need to show that the democracy mr. president, whistleblowers are of great importance to any society because they are people who put their own well-being at risk to bring hidden information to the public's attention. too often, such sacrifices are not appreciated by society to the degree they deserve and they are marginalized. whistleblower protection is therefore a necessity if we are to encourage citizens to disclose information of public interest without fear or hesitation. first of all, i am thinking here of a high-quality legislative framework at national level which should be adopted by all member states. but i must emphasize that absolute legal protection for whistleblowers such as the whistleblower and some of my colleagues are calling for is simply not possible. if we were to completely deny the other party, the one who is considered to be harmed, the right to seek justice in court, we would de facto be legalizing abuse of office, defamation and willful breach of contract. not all cases are the same, and it would not be wise to apply special rules to them. whether they apply to a particular case of a whistleblower is, however, a matter for the courts to decide.
mr. president, the idea of a minimum income as a negative income tax that would replace all other social transfers and drastically reduce administration is increasingly being abused by the economic left. the goal of the guaranteed minimum income, as conceived by milton friedman, is not to introduce another social measure at the expense of the productive part of the population, but just the opposite: to simplify the social system in such a way that the state provides the basic conditions for life for all, not just for some, while everyone else should work. since employment would not deprive people of their right to a minimum income, as is the case with various welfare measures, people would have a greater incentive to seek work in order to earn extra money. on the other hand, those who want to volunteer and do charitable work would have more time for such activities because they have a basic income guaranteed. unfortunately, this report is about something else entirely and i could not support it even if it did not seriously violate the principle of subsidiarity.

madam president, commissioner, i would like to congratulate my colleague, mr augustin díaz de mari, on his comprehensive report, which i regret to say has some reservations. member states located at the external borders of the european union are more exposed to security threats due to their long external borders and their openness to threats originating from third countries. i therefore consider that they should be given special attention within the regulation establishing an entry/exit system for the registration of entry and exit data of third-country nationals crossing the external borders of the european union. while this proposal for a regulation aimed at establishing an effective mechanism to further strengthen security within the european union, there are certain shortcomings in its implementation that could and do endanger the security of the european union. what i see as a key flaw in this proposal is the unequal treatment of all member states in the application of this mechanism, i.e. application in the schengen member states, while implementation is delayed in the countries located on the external borders of the european union, which include croatia. the application of this regulation in member states which are not part of the schengen area may affect the impossibility of registration of the stay of third-country nationals, and any omissions could thus pose a threat to the states at the external borders, their citizens, but also to everyone else. the uneven application of the regulation, the delay in application in the member states of the european union which are on the external borders, await full membership of the schengen area.
madam president, i congratulate the rapporteur and thank the commissioner and the rapporteur for mentioning the need to join the eea member states that are not yet in the schengen area as soon as possible. this is the system of promotion and security of the european union, but until it is applied in all the member states we cannot, unfortunately, talk about protecting the external borders of the european union but about protecting the schengen area. it is precisely because it is a system that will facilitate the fight against terrorism, organised crime, illegal migration and cross-border movements that all member states should be involved from the start of the operational application of the regulation to protect the external borders. only then will this regulation have full meaning and positive effects on the internal security of the european union and on cross-border traffic as well. croatia makes up 1350 km of the european union's external border and, if it is not in this system, it makes the entire european union vulnerable, not just croatia.
madam president, as the representative from croatia, the member state with the longest external land border, i am extremely pleased that the union is finally addressing the challenges of migration in a constructive way and is working to bring order to the system and to protect its external borders. but a good legal solution cannot produce results unless it is applied consistently, so i expect that, contrary to some announcements, the regulation will be implemented at the external borders from the start of its operational application, as its name implies. unequal application to schengen members and to countries like croatia, which are not yet schengen members, would undermine our efforts to protect the interests of all members without discrimination and to raise the level of security throughout the european union. the operational challenges that would arise from the uneven application of this regulation are also not negligible and i therefore consider that the equal application should be our absolute priority.
the republic of croatia has made all the necessary preparations and is ready to take a step further in its membership of the european family and become a member of the schengen area. this is good for croatia and for the other member states. in addition to the abolition of border controls with the member states of the european union and of passport controls at airports for travel to and from the schengen countries, entry into the schengen area will also strengthen security at the external borders of the european union. the longest external border of the european union is precisely the border of the republic of croatia with bosnia and herzegovina, and it is therefore in the european union's interest to protect this border even more strongly and as soon as possible. the commission has also proposed that the member states should be able to apply the schengen acquis to all persons who are not nationals of a member state, and to all persons who are not nationals of a member state. the european union must ensure equal treatment for all its member states and must not discriminate against countries on the way to schengen, and i therefore expect croatia to automatically become part of the entry/exit system in order to be able to collect and share data more effectively.

member of the commission. – mr president, rapporteur, honourable members, i wish to thank, first of all, the rapporteur ms mihaylova, and the shadow rapporteurs for their constructive approach to the proposal. following fruitful cooperation between the three institutions, in particular at the trilogue, i believe we have reached our common objective. regions in the european union are very diverse. we see that regions with urban characteristics sometimes work very differently from rural regions and face different challenges. while urban regions often offer better economic opportunities and infrastructure, they might face problems like pollution, traffic congestion, crime and overcrowding. rural regions, on the other hand, often lack infrastructure. these differences can also be observed when it comes to coastal regions, in which a large share of gdp is generated by the blue or ocean-centric economy. tercet will help our understanding of these phenomena by aggregating pre-existing data by typology. by doing so, it will back socio-economic territorial analysis and help to shape good evidence-based policy. the proposal is quite timely as it, for instance, includes a definition of cities that helps support the implementing of the urban agenda and city-focused policy. as of now, there is no agreed legal definition of what a city actually is in union law and this, as we all know, hinders a common understanding of the term and leads to the risk that we might not speak about the same thing when we talk about cities. to ensure a harmonised use of the term and a consistent application, it is vital that we achieve an unambiguous interpretation of the concept of cities, and of course the same applies for rural areas.
on behalf of the s&d club, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on her report. we know that eurostat publishes a wide range of regional statistics in accordance with the regulation establishing a common classification of territorial units for statistics, more popularly known as nuts. these statistics are widely used in decision-making at the level of the european union's regional policy and in determining the type of support from the cohesion fund for which european regions can apply. the current nuts regulation does not yet cover spatial typologies, which is addressed by this proposal. spatial typologies are also used in the european statistical system, in particular the degree of urbanisation and the definition of cities. the commission shall be empowered to adopt delegated acts in accordance with article 264 concerning the establishment of a european network of national experts in the field of public procurement. however, the ess still does not officially recognise these typologies due to their undefined legal status. the commission's explanatory memorandum to the proposal rightly states that territorial typologies have a major impact on regional policies, providing useful evidence that can help to develop adequate proposals and more integrated territorial approaches. this would give due recognition to the diversity of the regions of the european union. we need more statistical data because of the irrefutably significant differences between types of territory, which are not always the same in the member states. in response to this need, the proposed regulation aims to legally recognise regional typologies at the level of nuts 3, local administrative units and network cells, in order to integrate them into the existing nuts regulation. the proposed territorial typologies of the code are mainly: urban, rural, urban and coastal areas. this will allow thematic statistical regulations and pole initiatives to collect european statistics through territorial typologies in order to effectively identify the needs of individual areas in a fact-based manner. i support the initiative which therefore seeks to establish spatial typologies for the purposes of european statistics, thus ensuring, on the basis of definitions and statistical criteria, a harmonised and transparent application at the level of the european union and the member states. however, the division into urban, rural, urban and coastal areas is not comprehensive, and this was a great opportunity to recognise the spatial typologies of the european islands, which are home to more than 17 million inhabitants and whose special status is already recognised by article 174 of the treaty of lisbon. the islanders rightly ask how adequate policies can be created for islands if their status is not recognised. one of the big problems with all the initiatives that we're trying to implement is the lack of statistical data to base them on. the application of statistics at island level would allow a thorough overview of the characteristics of these areas, many of which are common, irrespective of the geographical position, size or level of development of the islands. such data should be comparable with other areas of typology and should greatly facilitate the development of targeted provisions of union legislation whenever necessary. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the eu's policy on the environment and its implementation in the member states, in particular through the implementation of the eu's policy on the environment and the implementation of the eu's policy on the environment. the adoption of this proposal is a good step forward, but we must continue to work on improving it in order to really demonstrate unity in our territorial diversity in practice.
on behalf of the ecr club. madam president, spatial typologies that have been used for years will be incorporated into the legal system of the union by this regulation, so that in the future we can rely on more accurate statistics to guide many european policies. i believe this will lead to a review of some of the statistical breakdowns used so far in order to make even better use of the funds. i will take the example of croatia, which is divided into two nuts ii regions in such a way that some of the poorest parts of pannonian croatia are placed in the same region as the most developed north-western part of the country, making it difficult for them to access funds. of course, that has to be corrected. i am pleased that this proposal will allow some non-managed units to deviate from the established thresholds due to specific geographical, socioeconomic, historical, cultural or environmental circumstances. this is particularly important for islands where we need to further facilitate participation in the funds in order to prevent further depopulation and reduce the economic marginalisation of the island population.

mr. president, since the victory of president dude and the law and justice party in the elections in poland, the european institutions have been trying to create the impression that this country has gone in the wrong direction, violates the fundamental human rights of its citizens and is on the verge of becoming a dictatorship. the reality is different, of course. all the polls show that poles are almost as enthusiastic about eu membership as they have been all these years, with the law and justice party enjoying the support of 45-50% of the electorate. it follows that the problem is something quite different from what we are being served. the pro-european polish people have a different vision of the union from the european establishment, and the current government enjoys strong support from poles as it seeks to put that vision into practice. if we continue to doff the poles from brussels, let no one be surprised tomorrow if poland becomes the new united kingdom. you can ignore the will of the people, and you can dismiss their ideas or their fears, only as long as they allow you to do so.
mr president, the harsh winters in the member states with a colder continental climate take their toll in human lives every year. every society has an obligation to care for its most vulnerable, and people without a roof over their heads in bad weather certainly are. i think it's wrong to make a distinction between asylum seekers and residents in this matter. the extreme cold equally threatens all people who lack shelter and heating, regardless of their origin or legal status. but when we're discussing the winter plan for asylum seekers, we also have to ask ourselves why we're taking in so many people that we can't adequately care for. how did we get to the point where we offer these people freedom, and then end up giving them sickness or death from the cold? that's a question that needs an urgent answer because winter is upon us.
mr. president, in the last century, europe was beset by two totalitarian regimes, nazi and communist. my father was a victim of both. he was deported to the nazi dachau concentration camp, and later imprisoned in the communist naked island camp. i want to be clear, though. while lenin's october revolution aimed at freedom and solidarity, hitler's nazism was rooted in racism and xenophobia. the october revolution had, besides soviet communism, where millions died, many variants in europe, but also in asia, africa and latin america. some of them are still alive today. and yugoslav, tito's communism initially went through many tragedies, produced many tragedies, but later his socialist version would still open up spaces for private property, self-managed socialism and freedom of movement, then throughout the divided europe. so unlike most people in this house, i have lived neither behind the iron curtain nor in a liberal democracy. i am absolutely certain that the best answer to both totalitarianisms is the european union.
mr. president, you can see how emotional these discussions are. you see how mr timmermans, who was lucky enough to live on the other side, understands and accepts the legacy that happened in europe, and you see how lucky we are to be able to live in a united europe today without totalitarianism. but we believe that uncovering the truth, condemning violations of basic human rights, and providing relief to victims and their families are prerequisites for a responsible confrontation with the past. our european common family has embraced the common heritage of civilisation and the values of the european union. we feel it is our responsibility to remind people today that european society is building its future in accordance with the european condemnation of all the fascist, communist and totalitarian systems which throughout europe over the last century have caused tragic suffering, persecution and the liquidation of political dissidents. and my homeland croatia, as a member of the european union, is an unquestioned part of the european circle of civilization and has no alternative to the process of confronting the past that other member states, also members of the eastern european communist bloc, have gone through. it's a necessary process in uncovering the truth. our common duty to the whole european generation, and especially to the young, is to open the way to a future without social divisions, based on mutual respect and respect for universal humanist values. we cannot allow the rise of radicalism and populism in europe to lead to a situation where the system becomes weak enough, where extremists can very suddenly enter the centre and where no one expects them.
mr. president, the main legacy of the bolshevik revolution is 100 million dead. some died of starvation, some in inhuman conditions in camps from various diseases and physical exhaustion, some from the effects of torture by the repressive apparatus, and some were simply liquidated as enemies of various communist regimes from 1917 to the present. all those who have the need to romanticize the bolshevik revolution and tell fairy tales about workers, equality and justice, first stop and remember these 100 million victims. and among them were many ordinary people, workers and dispossessed, and they were given a hard death by the communists in exchange for their hard life. unfortunately, we seem not to have learned our lesson, and today individuals who sleep with communist manuals under their pillows have great political power in some western countries, and in some even threaten to take power. we have a great responsibility to educate the new generations so that they can recognize the communist evil and resist it. and one more death in the name of a crazy ideology that has left behind 100 million corpses would be too much.

mr president, the final establishment of a fully functioning energy union will ensure that all member states and their citizens have a stable supply of energy and a competitive market for products and services in the energy sector. such a large-scale project for the future requires not only a joint political effort but also major investments in energy, transport and telecommunications infrastructure. the necessary infrastructure includes the lng terminal on the croatian island of krk, the construction of which i support and which i hope will be completed as soon as possible with the help of money from european funds. the energy union must be seen primarily as a common energy market that we are committed to making accessible to all citizens and economic operators, rather than as a tightly regulated community whose global competitiveness will be limited by an excessive regulatory burden.

mr. president, people with disabilities are one of the most vulnerable groups in society. every responsible society has an obligation to work every day to better integrate them, to raise their quality of life and to combat stereotypes. i welcome the report of mrs stevens, who has done a great job. i will emphasise in particular the part of the report which highlights the close link between the poverty and social exclusion of people with disabilities and the social rights and roles of their family members, who are often left to care for a particularly vulnerable family member on their own, without any compensation or professional assistance. i am very pleased that croatia is making serious progress in this area and i believe that this and similar reports will contribute to an even better understanding of people with disabilities and to raising common standards of their protection and assistance to the members of their families who care for them.

mr president, there are more than 80 million disabled people in the european union, and more than half of them are women. they urgently need an accessible environment free of prejudice. despite numerous international conventions and provisions of european law, including the existing european disability strategy, persons with disabilities still do not fully enjoy their civil and social rights. i would also like to point out that a large number of people with reduced mobility and disabilities in the european union live in multi-storey buildings without lifts. these people are prisoners of their apartments and often the only alternative is institutionalization. i call on the member states to use the european and national resources at their disposal to make life easier for these people. in conclusion, accessibility and non-discrimination for persons with disabilities should be promoted through the european union's structural funds.

madam president, according to a report by amnesty international, libya is full of atrocities. however, this cruelty is particularly directed at religious minorities, especially christian migrants and refugees who are subjected to abduction, torture, theft and physical attacks by daesh, criminal gangs and human traffickers, as well as state officials. the whole world witnessed this intolerance when a religious-motivated mass murder, motivated by hatred of christians, was committed on the road to the italian coast. even in moments of mortal danger shared equally by all on these rubber boards in the middle of the mediterranean, hatred and intolerance prevailed and resulted in the deaths of dozens of innocent people who prayed for the help and salvation of all present to the "hidden god". killing christians on the way to a christian europe is more than symbolic. knowing that billions of euros are constantly pouring into libya, the question is, for what purpose? what groups? with what consequences? europe should be more careful and more careful about which groups it financially supports.
written. today we are witnessing an increasing violation of human rights and fundamental freedoms around the world, often affecting the most vulnerable groups such as ethnic, linguistic and religious minorities, persons with disabilities, lgbti people, women, children, asylum seekers and migrants. the european union is based on a strong commitment to promoting and protecting human rights, democracy and the rule of law worldwide, and this report is a valuable instrument to provide a comprehensive overview of the eu's priorities, efforts and challenges in combating violations in third countries and to help identify further effective ways to address them. over the years, the union has adopted a number of important documents promoting and protecting human rights, and a range of diplomatic and cooperative tools to support the advancement of human rights around the world. the treaty of lisbon, the role of the council and the high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy, the strategic framework for human rights 2012-2021 and the human rights guidelines are important instruments by which the union promotes and protects human rights and fundamental freedoms. the eu should continue to actively support democratic human rights institutions and civil society in their promotion of democracy and should continue to actively combat all forms of violation of human rights, democracy and the rule of law within and beyond its borders.
on behalf of the ppe club. madam president, i would like to thank mr. alyn smith for his cooperation and mutual support. as this is our recommendation to the commission, the council and the high commissioner, i would like to stress once again that we have highlighted in our report hong kong as a major trading partner of the european union. of course, we strongly support the principle of one country, two systems, and the high degree of autonomy that hong kong has over china, which stemmed from the sino-british declaration twenty years ago. i also stress that we as european institutions need to provide support to all our trading and political partners. we know that hong kong has developed into a society where there is rule of law, freedom and high standards of safety, health and autonomy. with this report and this recommendation, we wanted to support the prosperity and rights of hong kong citizens to provide a model for democratic and political reform in china, with the aim of liberalisation and chinese society. respect for hong kong's autonomy is crucial for its further development and good relations with china and the european union should provide support in this regard. i would also stress the reform of universal suffrage so that future elections are fully democratic, fair, transparent, but that the bilateral dialogue with the european union in the various policy areas is continued, as we have confirmed by a large majority in the foreign affairs committee and i hope we will do so again tomorrow in the vote in parliament.
on behalf of the s&d club. madam president, i thank the rapporteur for her really good cooperation. this report is an important step in our further cooperation with hong kong. the one-state, two-system approach is the foundation of our relationship. twenty years have passed, thirty years are ahead of us. the european union has a close relationship with hong kong as an important trading partner, but our relationship needs to be upgraded, for example through better cooperation in education and research programmes. as for the situation in hong kong, beijing's intervention in its regulation is notable. the right of journalists to write freely is being restricted. we also witnessed the protest movements in hong kong. some are calling for independence, however, the official policy of the european union is the one-china, two-systems policy, which determines our cooperation. i also point to the problem of tax evasion and tax fraud. i support the call on the hong kong government to take more effective action against companies that facilitate tax evasion and tax fraud through their branches in the city. i hope that the majority of my colleagues will support this appeal in tomorrow's vote.
madam president, brexit will dominate this week's meeting, whether enough progress has been made to move to phase two. the joint statement was finally agreed last week, the stakes have risen, but so has the cost of divorce. i hope for faster progress in the second phase in which we will have to define the future relationship between the european union and the united kingdom. as a croatian mep, i particularly welcome the provision that croatian citizens will be treated equally with other citizens of the union as they are still subject to a temporary restriction on access to the labour market. i also support the historically important strengthening of joint defence cooperation, which has been agreed by as many as 25 members. it is good that president tusk has invited all the member states to the euro-area summit, given the importance of the agenda. but these are not the only issues of importance for the future of the european union. i am glad that social issues, culture and education have also found their place on the agenda.
mr president, a series of current events and security challenges, the refugee and migration crisis faced by the republic of croatia at the end of 2015, when more than half a million refugees passed through the country, have made border security one of the main priorities of the government of the republic of croatia. much has been accomplished since that time. in preparing for full membership of the schengen area, croatia has used almost all the resources of the schengen instrument: it has acquired ships, helicopters for border surveillance, built new and renovated existing border police stations, and thus contributed to the efforts to fully apply the schengen acquis. i believe that special attention should be paid to strengthening the external borders of the european union, which are also our national borders, especially if we take into account that croatia has the longest external border with the european union. all of the above points to the need for the republic of croatia to be included in the schengen area as soon as possible. i hope that croatia will meet the technical criteria as soon as possible and i expect that in 2019 it will be a full member of schengen.
mr president, the high migratory pressures and the terrorist threat have left a mark on schengen that is not even foreseen for the circumstances currently prevailing in europe and its immediate neighbourhood. the proven weaknesses, however, are not a reason to reject schengen, but a real incentive to promote it. what the schengen area has been since its first day - an area of trust, security and peace - we need more in europe today than at any time in the last twenty years. but the whole concept is pretty meaningless if it doesn't include all the countries with external borders. of course, there are very objective criteria that they have to meet, but now that that goal is within our grasp, we should push croatia, bulgaria and romania to join schengen as soon as possible. i therefore call on all those involved in the decision-making process to speed up the accession of these three countries to the schengen area.
mr president, mr commissioner, mr minister, i would like to thank my s&d colleagues for this very topical issue they have raised. as security threats increase, so does the need to create a single european security area, i.e. to include all eu countries in the schengen area. the disruption of the schengen area not only makes external border control and security more difficult, but also communication, the free movement of people, goods, capital and services, and thus weakens the economic strength of the european union, and in particular creates inequalities between member states and between citizens of the european union. i therefore welcome the announced entry of romania and bulgaria into the schengen area, as well as my country croatia as soon as they meet the conditions. i am pleased that both the minister and the commissioner have said that there are very few criteria left for croatia to fulfil in order to become a member of the schengen area and as i oppose all inequalities and unequal treatment of bulgaria and romania upon their entry into the schengen area, i expect that the decision on croatia's entry will be taken on the basis of criteria, on the basis of technical and administrative capacity, and not on the basis of political criteria, requirements and conditions.
mr president, first of all i would like to thank gianni pittelli and my group, on whose proposal we are discussing today the extension of schengen. the debate, quite rightly, involves not only bulgaria and romania but also croatia. i also thank commissioner avramopoulos for the commission's clear and encouraging positions. as many as one billion two hundred and fifty million journeys are recorded annually in the schengen area. the economic, political and integration effects of this great achievement of the european union need not be overstated. the rules for accession are clear it is a comprehensive list of technical criteria that a country must meet before joining. however, in the end, the crucial political agreement of all eu member states is needed. it is important to continue the implementation of the schengen acquis. this increases the level of trust between the schengen area members. the commission should also have confidence in those member states which have been working hard for a long time to meet all the criteria. i would point out that since croatia joined the schengen information system, in just four months, it has carried out 75 million checks and identified over 4000 violations. proof that the preparations already made are yielding results. in addition, croatia has the longest external land border of any member of the european union. we want to contribute to the security of our part of the external borders of the european union. we want the internal borders of the european union without the barbed wire that separates us from our neighbours today. it's up to croatia to meet all the criteria for schengen accession, which we expect by the end of next year. it is then up to the council to consider everything that has been done. the credibility of the european union will be clearly confirmed on this issue.
written. accession to the schengen area is one of the strategic priorities of the republic of croatia, which is approached with great seriousness and commitment, as the croatian external border is the longest external border of the eu. the european migration crisis and the problem of radicalisation and wahhabi settlements growing across the border in bih make the issue of enlargement and the need for croatia to join the schengen area urgent and necessary. the settlements serve as recruitment and training centres for radicalised members who are a direct threat to european security and therefore enhanced control and mutual cooperation and support with other schengen members is an important element of the security of the eu's external border with bih, which will also result in adequate sanctions and the prevention of illegal crossings of the external border. the republic of croatia aims to achieve technical readiness during 2018 so that the council can take a political decision on croatia's accession to the schengen area at the latest by the beginning of 2019, and in any event before the end of the current european commission's mandate. this would be a significant decision and send a positive message in favour of a united and strong europe, as only by working together can european citizens be adequately protected. i hereby express my support for the accession of bulgaria and romania to the schengen area and i believe that a europe without borders is the only way to a united and secure europe.
written. accession to the schengen area is a strategic priority for croatia. until we achieve this, we cannot talk about the equality of all eu members. the procedure for evaluating the application of the schengen acquis in croatia has been applied since july 2014. we submitted a declaration of readiness for evaluation to the european commission and provided answers to 384 questions and translations of all relevant legal acts in august 2015. the evaluations have so far been carried out in 6 areas (data protection, common visa policy, external border management, return and readmission, police cooperation and firearms regulations). for all the deficiencies identified and recommendations for corrective measures, croatia submitted action plans to address the deficiencies and most of the recommendations were met. under the schengen instrument, croatia had at its disposal eur 120 million, of which eur 116,7 million or 97,33% was spent. the aim is to achieve technical readiness during 2018, so that the council can take a political decision on croatia's accession to the schengen area no later than the beginning of 2019. in this respect, ladies and gentlemen, we also expect your support, the support of the european parliament, for this realistic plan.
mr president, europe's readiness to respond to natural disasters has always been a necessity and can be seen as complementary to the common security and defence policy. fires, floods, earthquakes and other disasters that cause loss of life and property require of us concrete actions to help citizens, concrete plans and forces to be used in such cases. the sympathy and solidarity that is most often expressed by the institutions, including this parliament, is good and desirable, but we need concrete action and concrete help for people. help and action that will make europe stronger than all the declarations, summits and statements of sympathy. the establishment of a resceu civil protection force at european level is therefore an encouraging message and a proposal that simply must be supported. i believe we are capable of making that proposal, providing good equipment, making a good plan for the use of force and, i sincerely hope, simple procedures for activating force. operational forces, if well organised, will be essential to enhance the capacity to respond to crises at european level or as a complement to national forces. of course, as in all other situations, the question of the justification of the investment will arise, and if we just analyse the damage from the fires last summer in the member states of the european union, we will see that the investment in civil protection is both necessary and justified.

am also grateful that this item has been brought up, that we can also speak about this in the european parliament, but unlike our colleagues from western europe, we in croatia still have fresh memories of a totalitarian regime that was neither fascist nor nazi, but that dealt with dissidents, individuals and groups that it considered a threat to itself. recent world history speaks of millions of deaths directly or indirectly linked to communist regimes and their symbols are equally unacceptable in 21st century europe as fascist and nazi. so i regret that in today's agenda item title, you, the proposers, did not include communism. of course i object to all totalitarian symbols and slogans. let me try to illustrate the situation. there are different interpretations of the past in the croatian public space, and the cultivation of a culture of remembrance of human rights violations and resistance to undemocratic regimes is not systematic and comprehensive. the current administrative and judicial practice regarding the display of symbols is not uniform. therefore, the government led by andrej plenkovic established the council for confronting the past, whose task is to make recommendations to the government and legally regulate the promotion of content glorifying undemocratic regimes. there is no totalitarian regime that deserves to be amnestied just because the victors write history. europe, and european croatia, should condemn with equal force all regimes that have implemented or are implementing discrimination on any basis, that are responsible for political persecution, individual or mass murder and repression of fundamental rights and freedoms. in any event, i welcome the fact that we are discussing this and i am also glad that the commission on behalf of the alde club. mr president, i found the impetus for this item on the agenda of our parliament in the sad but also dangerous fact that today, more than in previous decades, people who glorify nazism, fascism and their derivatives in various countries are gathering in the streets of european cities. we have seen recently what has been happening in warsaw, madrid, rome, sofia, zagreb, belgrade, and sadly in many other cities, large and small. there's too much hate manifesting around us for us to keep quiet. nazism originated in germany, but it was germany first and best of all that decided to punish all those who glorified nazism or used its symbols or slogans. italy did the same with fascism. unfortunately, there are still countries and authorities in the european union which are following the symbols and slogans of nazism, fascism or ustasha and chetnikism without serious sanctions and firm reactions. the european union was founded with the desire that war in europe never happen again. the evil of nazism that originated on european soil and spread to many other countries has been defeated, but it is obviously not eradicated. that's why we need to act. right here. that is why not only the member states, but also the european union itself, this parliament, this commission, must propose concrete legislative solutions at european level. i ask the commission and all of us here in this parliament to adopt a special resolution and other necessary acts for this parliamentary session, which will severely punish all those who glorify and use the symbols and slogans of the defeated criminal regimes.

mr. president, nazi and fascist symbols must not be welcome in public spaces and we must do everything in our power to make citizens fully aware of the horror of this ideology and renounce its legacy. we must do the same with the communist legacy which, unfortunately, is often celebrated and propagated in this house. but in order to successfully educate citizens about the benefits of democracy, we must believe in it ourselves. the european union has shown over and over again in the last 20 years that it only recognizes democratic processes when it likes the outcome. trying to change something at european level that has not passed through national parliaments is also a sham of democracy. despite noble intentions, it's dangerous to send a message to citizens that it's okay to bypass procedures and impose your will without democratic legitimacy. it is, believe it or not, the first step towards a new totalitarianism. and, mr. deputy, under fascism and nazism 17 million people were killed, under communism 90 million. that you didn't include communism in this discussion is terrifying to me.

mr. president, the debate about banning nazi and fascist symbols and slogans throughout the european union seems a little out of place to me today. the regime has been militarily defeated and condemned in numerous trials, declarations and laws at the national level. with the collapse of nazism and fascism, only half of europe, unfortunately, experienced freedom. the other half remained in power under the communist system and this other half did not gain its freedom until 45 years later. pressed by many and important european issues such as better education and employment for young people, stable economic growth, addressing the social issues of european citizens, the problems of migrants and many others, to name but a few, we are discussing today the banning of the symbol. it does look like a reverse-direction drive to me. it's like we're encouraging people to do things out of revolt that they wouldn't normally do. this debate is counterproductive because prohibition and repression have never brought solutions to anyone. quite the opposite. they have caused rebellion and revolt among young people who consider such debates to be the lowest priority in today's democratic europe. i wonder who needs that?
mr. president, children are the future of any society and we have a great responsibility to provide them with the safest and most carefree environment possible. we live in a time where it's not so easy because technology has advanced so much that it's become accessible to everyone, even the youngest children, and it's easier for predators to access their victims now than ever before. that is why good policies and awareness-raising campaigns are so important. the rapporteur recognises this and recommends a number of good measures to protect children and adolescents. but beyond raising awareness in a way that both children and parents are educated on how to use the internet without too much exposure to potential predators, i think prevention has to go in the direction of publicly registering convicted pedophiles, so that parents can see at any moment who is a threat to their children. as families live in the dark not knowing if there are convicted child sex offenders around them, it's going to be much harder to protect them.
